#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Point {
    int x, y;
    int id;
    Point() {}
    Point(int x, int y, int id) : x(x), y(y), id(id) {}
};

int cross(const Point& a, const Point& b, const Point& c) {
    return (b.x - a.x) * (c.y - b.y) - (b.y - a.y) * (c.x - b.x);
}

bool intersect(const Point& a1, const Point& a2, const Point& b1, const Point& b2) {
    int c1 = cross(a1, a2, b1);
    int c2 = cross(a1, a2, b2);
    if ((c1 > 0 && c2 > 0) || (c1 < 0 && c2 < 0)) return false;
    int c3 = cross(b1, b2, a1);
    int c4 = cross(b1, b2, a2);
    if ((c3 > 0 && c4 > 0) || (c3 < 0 && c4 < 0)) return false;
    return true;
}

int main() {
    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<Point> points;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int x, y;
        cin >> x >> y;
        points.emplace_back(x, y, i + 1);
    }
    string s;
    cin >> s;

    sort(points.begin(), points.end(), [](const Point& a, const Point& b) {
        return a.x < b.x || (a.x == b.x && a.y < b.y);
    });

    vector<int> perm;
    vector<bool> used(n, false);
    perm.push_back(0);
    perm.push_back(1);
    used[0] = used[1] = true;

    for (int i = 0; i < n - 2; ++i) {
        char turn = s[i];
        int a_idx = perm[perm.size() - 2];
        int b_idx = perm.back();
        const Point& a = points[a_idx];
        const Point& b = points[b_idx];

        int best_cr = 0;
        int best_candidate = -1;

        for (int j = 0; j < n; ++j) {
            if (used[j]) continue;
            const Point& c = points[j];
            int cr = cross(a, b, c);
            if ((turn == 'L' && cr > 0) || (turn == 'R' && cr < 0)) {
                if (best_candidate == -1 || abs(cr) > abs(best_cr)) {
                    best_cr = cr;
                    best_candidate = j;
                }
            }
        }

        if (best_candidate == -1) {
            cout << -1 << endl;
            return 0;
        }

        perm.push_back(best_candidate);
        used[best_candidate] = true;
    }

    vector<Point> poly;
    for (int idx : perm) {
        poly.push_back(points[idx]);
    }

    bool ok = true;
    for (int i = 0; i < n - 1 && ok; ++i) {
        for (int j = i + 1; j < n - 1; ++j) {
            if (abs(i - j) == 1) continue;
            const Point& a = poly[i];
            const Point& b = poly[i + 1];
            const Point& c = poly[j];
            const Point& d = poly[j + 1];
            if (intersect(a, b, c, d)) {
                ok = false;
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    if (!ok) {
        cout << -1 << endl;
    } else {
        for (int idx : perm) {
            cout << points[idx].id << " ";
        }
        cout << endl;
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#>5)≈1.5615=x. So it works.

Therefore, the Case 2 solution x=(sqrt(4a -3) -1)/2 works for a≥1. Also, check if for a=1, this gives x=0, which is the correct solution.

Therefore, putting this together:

The only real solutions are x=(sqrt(4a -3) -1)/2 for a≥1, and no solutions for a<1.

But let me verify for a=1. When a=1, x=(sqrt(4*1 -3)-1)/2=(sqrt(1)-1)/2=(1-1)/2=0. Which matches.

For a=3/4, which is less than 1, the sqrt(4a -3) is sqrt(-0.75), which is not real, so no solutions.

Therefore, the solution is x=(sqrt(4a -3) -1)/2 for a≥1, and no solutions otherwise.

But wait, let's check if this is the only solution. When we had the quartic equation, for a=2, there were two possible solutions from Case 2, but only one was valid. However, according to Case 2, x=(sqrt(4a -3) -1)/2 is the only valid solution when a≥1. Let's see.

But wait, when we factor the quartic equation in the case when a=2, we had (x² +x -1)(x² -x -2)=0. The solution x=(sqrt(5)-1)/2 comes from x² +x -1=0, which corresponds to x=( -1 ± sqrt(5) )/2. The positive solution is ( -1 + sqrt(5) )/2, which is the same as (sqrt(5) -1)/2. Which is the same as the solution we derived here for general a≥1, with a=2: sqrt(4*2 -3)=sqrt(5). So yes, general solution x=(sqrt(4a -3) -1)/2 is equivalent to the solution for a=2.

But how does this connect to the quadratic equation from Case 2? When we derived x=(sqrt(4a -3) -1)/2, it was from the quadratic equation x² +x +1 -a=0. So x=( -1 ± sqrt(4a -3) )/2. Taking the positive root gives x=( -1 + sqrt(4a -3) )/2.

But in the quartic equation for a=2, another factor gave x=2, which didn't work, and x=-1. So in general, the quartic factors into (x² +x +1 -a)(x² -x -a)=0. Wait, let me check.

Wait, in the case of a=2, the quartic factors into (x² +x -1)(x² -x -2). Which can be seen as (x² +x +1 -2)(x² -x -2). So generalizing, for general a, the quartic equation x⁴ -2a x² -x +a² -a=0 can be factored as (x² +x +1 -a)(x² -x -a)=0. Let me verify this:

Multiply (x² +x +1 -a)(x² -x -a):

= x²(x² -x -a) +x(x² -x -a) + (1 -a)(x² -x -a)

= x⁴ -x³ -a x² +x³ -x² -a x + (1 -a)x² - (1 -a)x -a(1 -a)

Simplify term by term:

x⁴

- x³ +x³ =0

- a x² -x² + (1 -a)x² = (-a -1 +1 -a)x² = (-2a)x²

- a x - (1 -a)x = (-a -1 +a)x = -x

-a(1 -a)= -a +a²

So total is x⁴ -2a x² -x +a² -a, which matches the quartic. So yes, the quartic factors as (x² +x +1 -a)(x² -x -a)=0.

Therefore, the solutions are from x² +x +1 -a=0 and x² -x -a=0.

The solutions to x² +x +1 -a=0 are x=(-1 ± sqrt(4a -3))/2.

The solutions to x² -x -a=0 are x=(1 ± sqrt(1 +4a))/2.

Now, considering x ≥0:

From x² +x +1 -a=0:

Solutions are x=(-1 + sqrt(4a -3))/2 and x=(-1 - sqrt(4a -3))/2. The second solution is negative (since sqrt(4a -3)≥0 when a≥3/4), so discard. The first solution x=(-1 + sqrt(4a -3))/2 is non-negative only if sqrt(4a -3) ≥1 ⇒4a -3 ≥1⇒a ≥1.

From x² -x -a=0:

Solutions are x=(1 + sqrt(1 +4a))/2 and x=(1 - sqrt(1 +4a))/2. The second solution is negative because sqrt(1 +4a)≥1, so 1 - sqrt(1 +4a) ≤0. The first solution x=(1 + sqrt(1 +4a))/2 is always positive. However, we need to check if this solution satisfies the original equation.

As we saw earlier, in the case of a=2, x=(1 + sqrt(9))/2=2 does not satisfy the original equation. Similarly, for a=1, x=(1 + sqrt(5))/2≈1.618, which is greater than a² -a=0 (since a=1, a² -a=0), so x=1.618 is outside the domain x ∈[0,0], which is invalid. Therefore, solutions from x² -x -a=0 are extraneous.

Therefore, the only valid solution is x=(-1 + sqrt(4a -3))/2 for a≥1. For a<1, there are no real solutions.

Thus, the solution is:

For a ≥1, x = [sqrt(4a -3) -1]/2

For a <1, no real solutions.

**Final Answer**
The real numbers \( x \) satisfying the equation are \( \boxed{\frac{\sqrt{4a - 3} - 1}{2}} \) for \( a \geq 1 \), and no solutions exist for \( 0 < a < 1 \).
</think>To determine all real numbers \( x \) which satisfy the equation \( x = \sqrt{a - \sqrt{a + x}} \) where \( a > 0 \) is a parameter, we proceed as follows:

1. **Initial Equation and Squaring:**
   \[
   x = \sqrt{a - \sqrt{a + x}}
   \]
   Square both sides to eliminate the outer square root:
   \[
   x^2 = a - \sqrt{a + x}
   \]

2. **Isolating the Inner Square Root:**
   \[
   \sqrt{a + x} = a - x^2
   \]
   Square both sides again to eliminate the inner square root:
   \[
   a + x = (a - x^2)^2
   \]

3. **Expanding and Rearranging:**
   Expand the right-hand side:
   \[
   a + x = a^2 - 2a x^2 + x^4
   \]
   Rearrange all terms to one side:
   \[
   x^4 - 2a x^2 - x + a^2 - a = 0
   \]

4. **Factoring the Quartic Equation:**
   The quartic equation can be factored into two quadratics:
   \[
   (x^2 + x + 1 - a)(x^2 - x - a) = 0
   \]

5. **Solving the Quadratic Equations:**
   - For \( x^2 + x + 1 - a = 0 \):
     \[
     x = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{4a - 3}}{2}
     \]
     The non-negative solution is:
     \[
     x = \frac{-1 + \sqrt{4a - 3}}{2}
     \]
     This solution is valid if \( 4a - 3 \geq<#sep#>).

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

const int BASE = 10;
const int MOD = 1e9 + 7;

struct Node {
    int hash;
    int len;
    char lazy;
};

vector<Node> tree;
vector<int> pow_base;
int inv_geo;

struct HashResult {
    int hash;
    int len;
};

void precompute(int n) {
    pow_base.resize(n + 1);
    pow_base[0] = 1;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        pow_base[i] = (pow_base[i-1] * BASE) % MOD;
    }
    int base_minus_1 = BASE - 1;
    inv_geo = 1;
    int exponent = MOD - 2;
    while (exponent) {
        if (exponent & 1) {
            inv_geo = (inv_geo * 1LL * base_minus_1) % MOD;
        }
        base_minus_1 = (base_minus_1 * 1LL * base_minus_1) % MOD;
        exponent >>= 1;
    }
}

void build(int node, int l, int r, const string &s) {
    if (l == r) {
        tree[node].hash = (s[l] - '0') % MOD;
        tree[node].len = 1;
        tree[node].lazy = -1;
        return;
    }
    int mid = (l + r) / 2;
    build(2*node, l, mid, s);
    build(2*node+1, mid+1, r, s);
    tree[node].len = tree[2*node].len + tree[2*node+1].len;
    tree[node].hash = ( (tree[2*node].hash * 1LL * pow_base[tree[2*node+1].len] ) % MOD + tree[2*node+1].hash ) % MOD;
    tree[node].lazy = -1;
}

void push(int node, int l, int r) {
    if (tree[node].lazy == -1) return;
    char c = tree[node].lazy;
    int len = tree[node].len;
    int val = ( (c * ( (pow_base[len] - 1 + MOD) % MOD )) % MOD * 1LL * inv_geo ) % MOD;
    tree[node].hash = val;
    if (l != r) {
        tree[2*node].lazy = c;
        tree[2*node+1].lazy = c;
    }
    tree[node].lazy = -1;
}

void update_range(int node, int l, int r, int ul, int ur, char c) {
    push(node, l, r);
    if (ur < l || ul > r) return;
    if (ul <= l && r <= ur) {
        tree[node].lazy = c;
        push(node, l, r);
        return;
    }
    int mid = (l + r) / 2;
    update_range(2*node, l, mid, ul, ur, c);
    update_range(2*node+1, mid+1, r, ul, ur, c);
    push(2*node, l, mid);
    push(2*node+1, mid+1, r);
    tree[node].hash = ( (tree[2*node].hash * 1LL * pow_base[tree[2*node+1].len] ) % MOD + tree[2*node+1].hash ) % MOD;
}

HashResult query_hash(int node, int l, int r, int ql, int qr) {
    push(node, l, r);
    if (qr < l || ql > r) {
        return {0, 0};
    }
    if (ql <= l && r <= qr) {
        return {tree[node].hash, tree[node].len};
    }
    int mid = (l + r) / 2;
    HashResult left = query_hash(2*node, l, mid, ql, qr);
    HashResult right = query_hash(2*node+1, mid+1, r, ql, qr);
    if (right.len == 0) {
        return left;
    } else if (left.len == 0) {
        return right;
    } else {
        int combined_hash = (left.hash * 1LL * pow_base[right.len] % MOD + right.hash) % MOD;
        return {combined_hash, left.len + right.len};
    }
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m, k;
    cin >> n >> m >> k;
    string s;
    cin >> s;

    precompute(n);
    tree.resize(4 * n);
    build(1, 0, n-1, s);

    for (int i = 0; i < m + k; ++i) {
        int type;
        cin >> type;
        if (type == 1) {
            int l, r;
            char c;
            cin >> l >> r >> c;
            l--; r--;
            update_range(1, 0, n-1, l, r, c - '0');
        } else {
            int l, r, d;
            cin >> l >> r >> d;
            l--; r--;
            int len = r - l + 1;
            if (d == len) {
                cout << "YES\n";
            } else {
                int part_len = len - d;
                int ql1 = l;
                int qr1 = l + part_len - 1;
                int ql2 = l + d;
                int qr2 = ql2 + part_len - 1;
                HashResult res1 = query_hash(1, 0, n-1, ql1, qr1);
                HashResult res2 = query_hash(1, 0, n-1, ql2, qr2);
                if (res1.hash == res2.hash) {
                    cout << "YES\n";
                } else {
                    cout << "NO\n";
                }
            }
        }
    }

    return 0;
}<#sep#> + 1 - a)(x^2 - x - a) = 0
   \]

5. **Solving the Quadratic Equations:**
   - For \( x^2 + x + 1 - a = 0 \):
     \[
     x = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{4a - 3}}{2}
     \]
     The non-negative solution is:
     \[
     x = \frac{-1 + \sqrt{4a - 3}}{2}
     \]
     This solution is valid if \( 4a - 3 \geq 1 \), which implies \( a \geq 1 \).

   - For \( x^2 - x - a = 0 \):
     \[
     x = \frac{1 \pm \sqrt{1 + 4a}}{2}
     \]
     The positive solution is:
     \[
     x = \frac{1 + \sqrt{1 + 4a}}{2}
     \]
     However, this solution is extraneous as it does not satisfy the original equation.

6. **Conclusion:**
   - For \( a \geq 1 \), the valid solution is:
     \[
     x = \frac{\sqrt{4a - 3} - 1}{2}
     \]
   - For \( 0 < a < 1 \), there are no real solutions.

### Final Answer
The real numbers \( x \) satisfying the equation are:
\[
\boxed{\frac{\sqrt{4a - 3} - 1}{2}} \quad \text{for} \quad a \geq 1,
\]
and no solutions exist for \( 0 < a < 1 \).<#sep#><1:m//=2;" → let's count:

#include <vector>
#include <queue>
#include <algorithm>
#include <cstdio>
using namespace std;

int pow_mod(int a, int b, int mod) {
    int res = 1;
    a %= mod;
    while (b > 0) {
        if (b % 2 == 1) {
            res = (1LL * res * a) % mod;
        }
        a = (1LL * a * a) % mod;
        b /= 2;
    }
    return res;
}

int mod_inverse(int a, int p) {
    return pow_mod(a, p-2, p);
}

int determinant(vector<vector<int>> M, int p) {
    int k = M.size();
    int det = 1;
    int swap_count = 0;
    for (int col = 0; col < k; ++col) {
        int pivot_row = -1;
        for (int row = col; row < k; ++row) {
            if (M[row][col] != 0) {
                pivot_row = row;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (pivot_row == -1) {
            return 0;
        }
        if (pivot_row != col) {
            swap(M[col], M[pivot_row]);
            swap_count++;
        }
        int inv_pivot = mod_inverse(M[col][col], p);
        for (int row = col + 1; row < k; ++row) {
            if (M[row][col] == 0) continue;
            int factor = (1LL * M[row][col] * inv_pivot) % p;
            for (int j = col; j < k; ++j) {
                M[row][j] = (M[row][j] - (1LL * factor * M[col][j]) % p + p) % p;
            }
        }
        det = (1LL * det * M[col][col]) % p;
    }
    if (swap_count % 2 == 1) {
        det = (p - det) % p;
    }
    return det;
}

int main() {
    int n, m, p;
    scanf("%d %d %d", &n, &m, &p);
    
    vector<vector<int>> adj(n+1);
    vector<int> in_degree(n+1, 0);
    vector<int> out_degree(n+1, 0);
    
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int a, b;
        scanf("%d %d", &a, &b);
        adj[a].push_back(b);
        in_degree[b]++;
        out_degree[a]++;
    }
    
    vector<int> sources, sinks;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        if (in_degree[i] == 0) {
            sources.push_back(i);
        }
        if (out_degree[i] == 0) {
            sinks.push_back(i);
        }
    }
    sort(sources.begin(), sources.end());
    sort(sinks.begin(), sinks.end());
    
    int k = sources.size();
    if (k == 0) {
        printf("0\n");
        return 0;
    }
    
    vector<int> top_order;
    vector<int> in_degree_copy = in_degree;
    queue<int> q;
    for (int i = 1; i <= n; ++i) {
        if (in_degree_copy[i] == 0) {
            q.push(i);
        }
    }
    while (!q.empty()) {
        int u = q.front();
        q.pop();
        top_order.push_back(u);
        for (int v : adj[u]) {
            if (--in_degree_copy[v] == 0) {
                q.push(v);
            }
        }
    }
    
    vector<vector<int>> mat(k, vector<int>(k, 0));
    for (int i = 0; i < k; ++i) {
        int s = sources[i];
        vector<int> dp(n+1, 0);
        dp[s] = 1;
        for (int u : top_order) {
            if (dp[u] == 0) continue;
            for (int v : adj[u]) {
                dp[v] = (dp[v] + dp[u]) % p;
            }
        }
        for (int j = 0; j < k; ++j) {
            mat[i][j] = dp[sinks[j]] % p;
        }
    }
    
    int det = determinant(mat, p);
    printf("%d\n", det);
    
    return 0;
}<#sep#>Given \(a, b, c > 0\) and \(abc = 1\), find the maximum value of the expression
\[
\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4} + \frac{b^2 + c^2}{b^4 + c^4} + \frac{c^2 + a^2}{c^4 + a^4}.
\]

<think>
Okay, so I have this problem here where I need to find the maximum value of the expression:

\[
\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4} + \frac{b^2 + c^2}{b^4 + c^4} + \frac{c^2 + a^2}{c^4 + a^4}
\]

given that \(a, b, c > 0\) and \(abc = 1\). Hmm, okay, let me think about how to approach this.

First, since the variables are all positive and their product is 1, maybe I can use some substitution to simplify things. Often in problems with \(abc = 1\), substituting variables as \(a = \frac{x}{y}\), \(b = \frac{y}{z}\), \(c = \frac{z}{x}\) can help, but I'm not sure if that's the way to go here. Alternatively, maybe using logarithms or Lagrange multipliers? But since it's an Olympiad-style problem, perhaps an inequality approach would be better, like AM-GM or Cauchy-Schwarz.

Let me first analyze the expression \(\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4}\). Maybe I can simplify this term first. Let's see, the denominator is \(a^4 + b^4\), which can be written as \((a^2)^2 + (b^2)^2\). The numerator is \(a^2 + b^2\). Hmm, so maybe I can factor or find a relationship between numerator and denominator.

Alternatively, notice that \(a^4 + b^4 \geq a^2b^2\) by AM-GM? Wait, actually, let me think. Let me check:

For any real numbers, \(a^4 + b^4 \geq a^2b^2\)?

Wait, actually, AM-GM on \(a^4\) and \(b^4\) gives \(\frac{a^4 + b^4}{2} \geq \sqrt{a^4b^4} = a^2b^2\), so \(a^4 + b^4 \geq 2a^2b^2\). So, \(a^4 + b^4\) is actually greater than or equal to twice \(a^2b^2\). So, the denominator is at least twice the product of squares. Hmm, not sure yet how this helps.

Alternatively, maybe I can write the fraction \(\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4}\) as \(\frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2}\) over something? Wait, let me see:

Wait, let's try to manipulate the fraction:

Let me consider \(\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4}\). Let me divide numerator and denominator by \(a^2b^2\):

So, numerator becomes \(\frac{1}{b^2} + \frac{1}{a^2}\), and denominator becomes \(\frac{a^4}{a^2b^2} + \frac{b^4}{a^2b^2} = \frac{a^2}{b^2} + \frac{b^2}{a^2}\).

Therefore, the fraction becomes \(\frac{\frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2}}{\frac{a^2}{b^2} + \frac{b^2}{a^2}}\).

Hmm, that might be a useful form. Let me denote \(x = \frac{a}{b}\) and \(y = \frac{b}{c}\) and \(z = \frac{c}{a}\), but not sure if that helps here. Alternatively, since \(abc = 1\), perhaps substituting variables such that \(a = \frac{x}{y}\), \(b = \frac{y}{z}\), \(c = \frac{z}{x}\), but maybe this complicates things further.

Alternatively, since the expression is symmetric in a cyclic way, maybe the maximum occurs when a = b = c. Let me test that.

If a = b = c, then since \(abc =1\), each variable is 1. So plugging in a = b = c =1:

Each term becomes \(\frac{1 +1}{1 +1} = 1\), so the total is 3. So the expression equals 3 when a = b = c =1. So that's a candidate. But is that the maximum?

Wait, maybe there's a higher value when variables are unequal? Let me test some cases.

Suppose I let a approach 0, but since a, b, c are positive and their product is 1, if a approaches 0, then at least one of b or c must approach infinity. Let me see what happens to the expression in such a case.

Suppose a approaches 0, then since abc =1, then bc must approach infinity. Let me set a approaching 0, so let me let a = ε, very small, so then bc = 1/ε. Let me set b = c = sqrt(1/ε). Then, as ε approaches 0, b and c go to infinity.

Compute each term:

First term: (a² + b²)/(a⁴ + b⁴) ≈ (b²)/(b⁴) = 1/b² ≈ ε, which approaches 0.

Second term: (b² + c²)/(b⁴ + c⁴). Since b = c, this is (2b²)/(2b⁴) = 1/b² ≈ ε, same as first term.

Third term: (c² + a²)/(c⁴ + a⁴) ≈ same as first term, approaching 0.

So total approaches 0, which is worse. So making one variable very small and others large reduces the sum. Hmm, so maybe extremes are bad. What about making one variable large and another small?

Wait, maybe if two variables are equal and the third is different? Let me try with a = b, then c = 1/a².

Let me set a = b = t, so c = 1/t².

Compute the expression:

First term: (t² + t²)/(t⁴ + t⁴) = (2t²)/(2t⁴) = 1/t².

Second term: (t² + (1/t²)^2)/(t⁴ + (1/t²)^4). Let's compute:

Numerator: t² + 1/t⁴.

Denominator: t⁴ + 1/t^8.

Hmm, that seems complicated. Let me compute with specific numbers. Let me set t = 2, so c = 1/4.

First term: 1/(2)^2 = 1/4.

Second term: (4 + 1/16)/(16 + 1/256) = (65/16)/(4097/256) = (65/16)*(256/4097) = (65*16)/4097 ≈ 1040/4097 ≈ 0.254.

Third term: ( (1/4)^2 + 4 ) / ( (1/4)^4 + 16 ) = (1/16 + 4)/(1/256 +16) = (65/16)/(4097/256) same as second term, so ≈0.254.

Total is approx 0.25 + 0.254 +0.254 ≈ 0.758, which is way less than 3. Hmm, so that's worse. Maybe t =1 gives the maximum here.

Alternatively, let me try t =1, then c =1, so all variables are 1, so total is 3. So better.

What if I set a =2, b =2, then c =1/4. Wait, similar to above. Hmm, perhaps the maximum is indeed at a =b =c. Let me try another case where variables are not equal but not too extreme.

Suppose a =2, b=1/2, then c must be 1/(2*(1/2)) =1. So c<#sep#> string:

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <queue>
#include <unordered_set>
#include <algorithm>
#include <climits>

using namespace std;

struct State {
    int pos[3];
    bool used_move[3];
    bool used_grab[3];
    bool used_throw[3];
    int parent[3];

    bool operator==(const State& other) const {
        for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
            if (pos[i] != other.pos[i]) return false;
            if (used_move[i] != other.used_move[i]) return false;
            if (used_grab[i] != other.used_grab[i]) return false;
            if (used_throw[i] != other.used_throw[i]) return false;
            if (parent[i] != other.parent[i]) return false;
        }
        return true;
    }

    struct Hash {
        size_t operator()(const State& s) const {
            size_t h = 0;
            for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
                h ^= hash<int>()(s.pos[i]) << (i*4);
                h ^= hash<bool>()(s.used_move[i]) << (i*4 + 1);
                h ^= hash<bool>()(s.used_grab[i]) << (i*4 + 2);
                h ^= hash<bool>()(s.used_throw[i]) << (i*4 + 3);
                h ^= hash<int>()(s.parent[i]) << (i*4 + 4);
            }
            return h;
        }
    };
};

void normalize(State& s) {
    for (int c = 0; c < 3; ++c) {
        int root = c;
        while (s.parent[root] != -1) {
            root = s.parent[root];
        }
        s.pos[c] = s.pos[root];
    }
}

bool is_valid(const State& s) {
    vector<int> root(3);
    for (int c = 0; c < 3; ++c) {
        root[c] = c;
        while (s.parent[root[c]] != -1) {
            root[c] = s.parent[root[c]];
        }
    }
    for (int c1 = 0; c1 < 3; ++c1) {
        for (int c2 = c1 + 1; c2 < 3; ++c2) {
            if (root[c1] != root[c2] && s.pos[c1] == s.pos[c2]) {
                return false;
            }
        }
    }
    return true;
}

struct Character {
    int pos, move_range, throw_range;
};

int main() {
    Character chars[3];
    for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
        cin >> chars[i].pos >> chars[i].move_range >> chars[i].throw_range;
    }

    State initial;
    for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
        initial.pos[i] = chars[i].pos;
        initial.used_move[i] = false;
        initial.used_grab[i] = false;
        initial.used_throw[i] = false;
        initial.parent[i] = -1;
    }
    normalize(initial);

    unordered_set<State, State::Hash> visited;
    queue<State> q;
    int max_pos = 0;

    if (is_valid(initial)) {
        q.push(initial);
        visited.insert(initial);
        max_pos = max({initial.pos[0], initial.pos[1], initial.pos[2]});
    }

    while (!q.empty()) {
        State s = q.front();
        q.pop();

        max_pos = max({max_pos, s.pos[0], s.pos[1], s.pos[2]});

        for (int i = 0; i < 3; ++i) {
            // Move action
            if (!s.used_move[i] && s.parent[i] == -1) {
                bool has_child = false;
                for (int j = 0; j < 3; ++j) {
                    if (j != i && s.parent[j] == i) {
                        has_child = true;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                if (has_child) continue;

                int current_pos = s.pos[i];
                int range = chars[i].move_range;
                for (int dx = -range; dx <= range; ++dx) {
                    int new_pos = current_pos + dx;
                    if (new_pos < 1) continue;

                    bool occupied = false;
                    for (int j = 0; j < 3; ++j) {
                        if (j != i && s.parent[j] == -1 && s.pos[j] == new_pos) {
                            occupied = true;
                            break;
                        }
                    }
                    if (occupied) continue;

                    State new_s = s;
                    new_s.used_move[i] = true;
                    new_s.pos[i] = new_pos;
                    normalize(new_s);
                    if (is_valid(new_s) && visited.find(new_s) == visited.end()) {
                        visited.insert(new_s);
                        q.push(new_s);
                    }
                }
            }

            // Grab action
            if (!s.used_grab[i] && s.parent[i] == -1) {
                bool has_child = false;
                for (int j = 0; j < 3; ++j) {
                    if (j != i && s.parent[j] == i) {
                        has_child = true;
                        break;
                    }
                }
                if (has_child) continue;

                for (int j = 0; j < 3; ++j) {
                    if (j == i || s.parent[j] != -1) continue;
                    if (abs(s.pos[i] - s.pos[j]) == 1) {
                        State new_s = s;
                        new_s.parent[j] = i;
                        new_s.used_grab[i] = true;
                        normalize(new_s);
                        if (is_valid(new_s) && visited.find(new_s) == visited.end()) {
                            visited.insert(new_s);
                            q.push(new_s);
                        }
                    }
                }
            }

            // Throw action
            if (!s.used_throw[i]) {
                vector<int> children;
                for (int j = 0; j < 3; ++j) {
                    if (s.parent[j] == i) children.push_back(j);
                }
                if (children.empty()) continue;

                for (int j : children) {
                    int range = chars[i].throw_range;
                    for (int d = 1; d <= range; ++d) {
                        for (int dir : {1, -1}) {
                            int x = s.pos[i] + dir * d;
                            if (x < 1) continue;

                            vector<int> hierarchy;
                            hierarchy.push_back(j);
                            stack<int> st;
                            st.push(j);
                            while (!st.empty()) {
                                int c = st.top();
                                st.pop();
                                for (int k = 0; k < 3; ++k) {
                                    if (k != c && s.parent[k] == c) {
                                        hierarchy.push_back(k);
                                        st.push(k);
                                    }
                                }
                            }

                            bool occupied = false;
                            for (int k = 0; k < 3; ++k) {
                                if (find(hierarchy.begin(), hierarchy.end(), k) == hierarchy.end() && s.pos[k] == x) {
                                    occupied = true;
                                    break;
                                }
                            }
                            if (occupied) continue;

                            State new_s = s;
                            new_s.used_throw[i] = true;
                            new_s.parent[j] = -1;

                            vector<int> new_hierarchy;
                            new_hierarchy.push_back(j);
                            stack<int> new_st;
                            new_st.push(j);
                            while (!new_st.empty()) {
                                int c = new_st.top();
                                new_st.pop();
                                for (int k = 0; k < 3; ++k) {
                                    if (new_s.parent[k] == c) {
                                        new_hierarchy.push_back(k);
                                        new_st.push(k);
                                    }
                                }
                            }

                            for (int c : new_hierarchy) {
                                new_s.pos[c] = x;
                            }
                            normalize(new_s);

                            if (is_valid(new_s) && visited.find(new_s) == visited.end()) {
                                visited.insert(new_s);
                                q.push(new_s);
                            }
                        }
                    }
                }
            }
        }
    }

    cout << max_pos << endl;

    return 0;

#include <bits/stdc++.h>

using namespace std;

vector<vector<int>> adj;
vector<int> degrees;

int find_furthest(int start) {
    int n = adj.size() - 1;
    vector<int> dist(n + 1, -1);
    queue<int> q;
    q.push(start);
    dist[start] = 0;
    int furthest = start, max_dist = 0;
    while (!q.empty()) {
        int u = q.front();
        q.pop();
        for (int v : adj[u]) {
            if (dist[v] == -1) {
                dist[v] = dist[u] + 1;
                if (dist[v] > max_dist) {
                    max_dist = dist[v];
                    furthest = v;
                }
                q.push(v);
            }
        }
    }
    return furthest;
}

vector<int> get_path(int start, int end) {
    int n = adj.size() - 1;
    vector<int> parent(n + 1, -1);
    queue<int> q;
    q.push(start);
    parent[start] = -1;
    while (!q.empty()) {
        int u = q.front();
        q.pop();
        if (u == end) break;
        for (int v : adj[u]) {
            if (parent[v] == -1) {
                parent[v] = u;
                q.push(v);
            }
        }
    }
    vector<int> path;
    int current = end;
    while (current != start) {
        path.push_back(current);
        current = parent[current];
    }
    path.push_back(start);
    reverse(path.begin(), path.end());
    return path;
}

bool is_valid(int root) {
    int n = adj.size() - 1;
    vector<int> level(n + 1, -1);
    queue<int> q;
    q.push(root);
    level[root] = 0;
    bool valid = true;
    while (!q.empty() && valid) {
        int size = q.size();
        int expected_degree = -1;
        for (int i = 0; i < size; ++i) {
            int u = q.front();
            q.pop();
            if (expected_degree == -1) {
                expected_degree = degrees[u];
            } else if (degrees[u] != expected_degree) {
                valid = false;
                break;
            }
            for (int v : adj[u]) {
                if (level[v] == -1) {
                    level[v] = level[u] + 1;
                    q.push(v);
                }
            }
        }
    }
    return valid;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    cin >> n;

    if (n == 1) {
        cout << 1 << endl;
        return 0;
    }

    adj.resize(n + 1);
    degrees.resize(n + 1, 0);

    for (int i = 0; i < n - 1; ++i) {
        int u, v;
        cin >> u >> v;
        adj[u].push_back(v);
        adj[v].push_back(u);
        degrees[u]++;
        degrees[v]++;
    }

    int u = find_furthest(1);
    int v = find_furthest(u);
    vector<int> path = get_path(v, u);
    int len = path.size();

    vector<int> centers;
    if (len % 2 == 1) {
        centers.push_back(path[len / 2]);
    } else {
        centers.push_back(path[len / 2 - 1]);
        centers.push_back(path[len / 2]);
    }

    for (int c : centers) {
        if (is_valid(c)) {
            cout << c << endl;
            return 0;
        }
    }

    cout << -1 << endl;

    return 0;

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

bool is_gp(const vector<int>& a) {
    int n = a.size();
    if (n <= 1) return true;
    if (a[0] == 0) {
        for (int x : a) if (x != 0) return false;
        return true;
    } else {
        if (a[1] == 0) {
            for (int i = 2; i < n; ++i) if (a[i] != 0) return false;
            return true;
        } else {
            long long num = a[1], den = a[0];
            for (int i = 1; i < n - 1; ++i) {
                if (a[i + 1] * den != a[i] * num) return false;
            }
            return true;
        }
    }
}

bool check_after_delete(const vector<int>& a, int skip) {
    int n = a.size() - 1;
    if (n <= 1) return true;
    int first = 0;
    if (skip == 0) first = 1;
    int second = first + 1;
    while (second < (int)a.size() && second == skip) ++second;
    if (second >= (int)a.size()) return true;
    int a0 = a[first], a1 = a[second];
    if (a0 == 0) {
        for (int i = 0; i < (int)a.size(); ++i) {
            if (i == skip || i < first || i == first) continue;
            if (a[i] != 0) return false;
        }
        return true;
    } else {
        if (a1 == 0) {
            for (int i = 0; i < (int)a.size(); ++i) {
                if (i == skip || i < second || i == second) continue;
                if (a[i] != 0) return false;
            }
            return true;
        } else {
            long long num = a1, den = a0;
            int prev = a1;
            for (int i = second + 1; i < (int)a.size(); ++i) {
                if (i == skip) continue;
                if (a[i] * den != prev * num) return false;
                prev = a[i];
            }
            return true;
        }
    }
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);
    int n;
    cin >> n;
    vector<int> a(n);
    for (int& x : a) cin >> x;
    if (is_gp(a)) {
        cout << 0 << endl;
        return 0;
    }
    if (n == 2) {
        cout << 1 << endl;
        return 0;
    }
    int first_error = -1;
    if (a[0] == 0) {
        for (int i = 1; i < n; ++i) {
            if (a[i] != 0) {
                first_error = i - 1;
                break;
            }
        }
    } else if (a[1] == 0) {
        for (int i = 2; i < n; ++i) {
            if (a[i] != 0) {
                first_error = 1;
                break;
            }
        }
    } else {
        long long num = a[1], den = a[0];
        for (int i = 1; i < n - 1; ++i) {
            if (a[i + 1] * den != a[i] * num) {
                first_error = i;
                break;
            }
        }
        if (first_error == -1 && a[n-1] * den != a[n-2] * num) first_error = n-2;
    }
    vector<int> candidates = {0, 1, n - 1};
    if (first_error != -1) {
        candidates.push_back(first_error);
        if (first_error + 1 < n) candidates.push_back(first_error + 1);
    }
    sort(candidates.begin(), candidates.end());
    candidates.erase(unique(candidates.begin(), candidates.end()), candidates.end());
    for (int k : candidates) {
        if (k < 0 || k >= n) continue;
        if (check_after_delete(a, k)) {
            cout << 1 << endl;
            return 0;
        }
    }
    cout << 2 << endl;
    return 0;
}<#sep#>). Let me consider that fraction. Let me set t = a², s = b², so the term becomes (t + s)/(t² + s²). Maybe find the maximum of (t + s)/(t² + s²) over t, s >0. Hmm, but with the constraint that variables are connected via abc=1.

Alternatively, for each pair, can we find an upper bound for \(\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4}\)?

Let me see:

Let me note that \(a^4 + b^4 \geq a^2b^2\) (from AM-GM: \(a^4 + b^4 \geq 2a^2b^2\), so even better). Therefore,

\[
\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4} \leq \frac{a^2 + b^2}{2a^2b^2} = \frac{1}{2}\left( \frac{1}{a^2} + \frac{1}{b^2} \right)
\]

Hmm, but since \(abc =1\), so \(ab = 1/c\), so \(1/(a^2) +1/(b^2) = c^2(a^2 + b^2)\). Wait, maybe this is not helpful.

Alternatively, maybe using Cauchy-Schwarz:

\[
(a^4 + b^4)(1 +1) \geq (a^2 + b^2)^2 \implies \frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4} \leq \frac{2}{a^2 + b^2}
\]

Therefore, each term is ≤ 2/(a² + b²). So the sum is ≤ 2(1/(a² +b²) +1/(b² +c²)+1/(c² +a²)). Hmm, but not sure how to relate this to the given condition.

Alternatively, since we need to maximize the sum, maybe each term is maximized when a =b, so the maximum of each individual term is 1/(a²) when a =b? Wait, when a =b, then the term is (2a²)/(2a⁴) =1/a². So if a =b =c =1, each term is 1, so sum is 3. Alternatively, if a =b but c different, then each term where a and b are equal gives 1/a², but the other terms may be smaller. So perhaps the maximum occurs when all variables are equal.

Alternatively, let me consider using the substitution with a = x/y, b = y/z, c = z/x. Then abc =1. Then substituting into the expression, but that might complicate the terms.

Alternatively, let me use Lagrange multipliers. Let me set variables a,b,c>0 with abc=1. Let me denote the function to maximize as:

F(a,b,c) = \(\frac{a^2 + b^2}{a^4 + b^4} + \frac{b^2 + c^2}{b^4 + c^4} + \frac{c^2 + a^2}{c^4 + a^4}\).

Subject to the constraint G(a,b,c)=abc -1=0.

Set up Lagrangian: L = F(a,b,c) - λ(abc -1).

Then take partial derivatives with respect to a, b, c, set to zero.

This might be complicated, but let's try for symmetry. Suppose that the maximum occurs at a =b =c=1, so check if that is a critical point.

Compute partial derivative of F with respect to a:

First term: d/da [ (a² + b²)/(a⁴ + b⁴) ]

Let me compute derivative of first term:

Let f(a) = (a² + b²)/(a⁴ + b⁴). Then f’(a) = [2a(a⁴ + b⁴) - (a² + b²)(4a³)]/(a⁴ + b⁴)^2

= [2a(a⁴ + b⁴) -4a³(a² + b²)] / (denominator)

Factor numerator:

2a [ (a⁴ + b⁴) -2a²(a² + b²) ]

=2a[ a⁴ + b⁴ -2a⁴ -2a²b² ]

=2a[ -a⁴ + b⁴ -2a²b² ]

Hmm, at a =b=1, this becomes:

2*1[ -1 +1 -2*1*1 ]= 2*(-2)= -4.

Second term in F: the term with b and c. The derivative with respect to a is 0 unless it's the third term.

Third term: (c² +a²)/(c⁴ +a⁴). Its derivative with respect to a is similar to the first term.

Let me compute derivative of third term:

Let h(a) = (c² +a²)/(c⁴ +a⁴). Then h’(a) = [2a(c⁴ +a⁴) - (c² +a²)(4a³)]/(denominator)^2

=2a(c⁴ +a⁴) -4a³(c² +a²) all over denominator squared.

Factor numerator:

2a[ c⁴ +a⁴ -2a²(c² +a²) ]

=2a[ c⁴ +a⁴ -2a²c² -2a⁴ ]

=2a[ -a⁴ +c⁴ -2a²c² ]

So at a =c=1, this becomes:

2*1[ -1 +1 -2*1*1 ]=2*(-2)= -4.

Now, the second term in F is (b² +c²)/(b⁴ +c⁴), derivative with respect to a is 0.

Thus, total derivative of F w.r. to a at a =b =c=1:

First term's derivative: -4 (from first term) + third term's derivative: -4, so total -8?

Wait, but wait, actually, let me recast:

Wait, when taking partial derivative of F w.r. to a, the first term has derivative as computed above (with a and b). Since at a =b =c=1, the first term's derivative is -4, the third term's derivative is -4, and the second term has 0. So total derivative is -4 + (-4) = -8? But Lagrangian multiplier equation would be partial derivative of L w.r. to a = 0:

dF/da - λ*(bc) =0. Since abc=1, so bc =1/a=1. So,

-8 - λ*(1) =0 => λ= -8.

Similarly, partial derivatives with respect to b and c would also give -8 -λ=0, so same λ. So it is consistent, so a =b =c=1 is a critical point.

But is it a maximum?

To confirm, we need to check the second derivative or see behavior, but given that when variables are unequal the value is lower, as in previous examples, it's likely that this critical point is the maximum.

Therefore, the maximum value is 3, achieved when a =b =c =1.

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <unordered_map>
#include <algorithm>
using namespace std;

struct Ratio {
    long long num, den;

    Ratio() : num(0), den(1) {}
    Ratio(long long n, long long d) {
        long long g = __gcd(n, d);
        if (g != 0) {
            n /= g;
            d /= g;
        }
        if (d < 0) {
            n = -n;
            d = -d;
        }
        num = n;
        den = d;
    }

    bool operator==(const Ratio& other) const {
        return num == other.num && den == other.den;
    }
};

namespace std {
    template<> struct hash<Ratio> {
        size_t operator()(const Ratio& r) const {
            return hash<long long>()(r.num) ^ (hash<long long>()(r.den) << 1);
        }
    };
}

vector<Ratio> compute_ratios(int a, int b, int c, int d) {
    vector<Ratio> res(2);

    long long x_num = a, x_den = b;
    long long y_num = c, y_den = d;

    // Compute r1: y / (x + 1)
    long long r1_num = y_num * (x_den);
    long long r1_den = y_den * (x_num + x_den);
    res[0] = Ratio(r1_num, r1_den);

    // Compute r2: (y + 1) / x
    long long r2_num = (y_num + y_den) * x_den;
    long long r2_den = y_den * x_num;
    res[1] = Ratio(r2_num, r2_den);

    return res;
}

int main() {
    ios_base::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n;
    cin >> n;

    vector<vector<Ratio>> points(n);
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        int a, b, c, d;
        cin >> a >> b >> c >> d;
        points[i] = compute_ratios(a, b, c, d);
    }

    unordered_map<Ratio, int> ratio_available;
    vector<vector<Ratio>> point_available(n);
    vector<pair<int, int>> result;

    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        auto& rs = points[i];
        Ratio r1 = rs[0];
        Ratio r2 = rs[1];

        bool paired = false;

        if (ratio_available.count(r1) && ratio_available[r1] != -1) {
            int j = ratio_available[r1];
            ratio_available[r1] = -1;
            result.emplace_back(j + 1, i + 1);
            for (auto& r : point_available[j]) {
                if (ratio_available[r] == j) {
                    ratio_available[r] = -1;
                }
            }
            point_available[j].clear();
            paired = true;
        } else if (ratio_available.count(r2) && ratio_available[r2] != -1) {
            int j = ratio_available[r2];
            ratio_available[r2] = -1;
            result.emplace_back(j + 1, i + 1);
            for (auto& r : point_available[j]) {
                if (ratio_available[r] == j) {
                    ratio_available[r] = -1;
                }
            }
            point_available[j].clear();
            paired = true;
        }

        if (!paired) {
            ratio_available[r1] = i;
            ratio_available[r2] = i;
            point_available[i].push_back(r1);
            point_available[i].push_back(r2);
        }
    }

    cout << result.size() << '\n';
    for (auto& p : result) {
        cout << p.first << ' ' << p.second << '\n';
    }

    return 0;

i=1
while True:
    if i%15 == 0:
        print("FizzBuzz")
    elif i%3 == 0:
        print("Fizz")
    elif i%5 == 0:
        print("Buzz")
    else:
        print(i)
    i += 1

We can represent its output as an infinite array of chars/bytes (assuming \n line terminators) that looks like the following:
['1', '\n', '2', '\n', 'F', 'i', 'z', 'z', '\n', '4', '\n', 'B', 'u', 'z', 'z', '\n', 'F', 'i', 'z', 'z', ...]

Your task is to write the shortest function or program that returns the character at the Nth index of this array.
Sounds easy? Your code should also work for extremely large values of N, for example 1e100.
It is possible to calculate the Nth byte of this output array in O(log(n)) time (or possibly better) - how is left as an exercise for the reader.
Your solution must run in O(poly(log(n))) time (or better). There is no hard execution time limit, but as a rough guideline you should be able to solve the N=1e100 case almost instantaneously.
This is code-golf: the shortest code in bytes wins.
Test cases:
f(0)      == '1'
f(1)      == '\n'
f(2)      == '2'
f(10)     == '\n'
f(100)    == '\n'
f(1000)   == 'z'
f(100000) == 'F'
f(1e10)   == '5'
f(1e100)  == '0'

Some more, randomly generated testcases: https://gist.github.com/DavidBuchanan314/65eccad5032c427218eb9c269b55e869
Note: these test cases are correct to the best of my knowledge. If you have contradictory findings, I am open to discussion.


gpt: <think> Okay, so I need to find the Nth character in the infinite array produced by the FizzBuzz code. The challenge is to do this efficiently even for very large N like 1e100. Hmm, right now the problem is essentially figuring out which number the N-th character corresponds to and then determining the character itself. Since the output is a sequence of lines, each line can take varying lengths (from 1 digit up to "FizzBuzz" which is 8 characters). But how do I compute this efficiently?

First, I should model the entire stream of characters. Each number i contributes a certain number of characters followed by a newline. Let me think about how many characters each line contributes. For instance:

- Numbers not divisible by 3 or 5: just their string representation plus a newline. The string can be 1-... well, depends on i. The digits of i determine the length here.

- If divisible by 3: "Fizz" (4) plus newline if not divisible by 5. Or combined with 5 to get "FizzBuzz" (8) plus newline. So for i which is multiples of 15: 8 + 1 (the newline), others are 4 or 5?

Wait, the actual code uses print statements with \n, so each line ends with a newline. So each line contributes chars for the output string plus a newline. So for each number i, the number of characters added is (length of output string (e.g., 'Fizz' is 4)) plus 1 for the newline.

Wait the test case shows: For N=0, it's '1', then N=1 is '\n'. So first number contributes "1\n", which is 2 chars. Wait the example given in the problem says the array starts with ['1', '\n', '2', ...], so the second element is the newline, so that’s right. Each line has the string for the number followed by a newline.

Hence for each i, the number of characters added is (number of digits of the number string/"FizzBuzz"/etc.) + 1 (for the newline). The actual numbers: for i's, "F", "i", etc: but each number's contribution is fixed.

So to find the Nth character in the sequence, we need to skip through all the characters before the target until we reach the right position. The challenge is, for large N, doing this step by step is impossible because it would take O(N) time which is way too slow.

Therefore, we need to find a mathematical way to compute how many characters are contributed up to a certain number, and home in on the exact line (number i) and the correct position in that line's contribution. Then, once we find that line, we can figure out the exact character.

First, the steps would be:

1. Find the largest i such that the total chars up to and including number i is less than or equal to N.

2. Then, find the difference between N and that total to position within the next number's output.

Wait, but the numbering here is that up to number 1, the total is 2 (the first two chars: "1\n"). So the cumulative count Upto(1) = 2. The next number (i=2) adds "2" followed by \n, so Upto(2) is 2 + 2 (positions 3 and 4?), so total 4. Let me think of total up to n as T(n). We need T(n) <= N < T(n+1). Then the nth line is the right one.

Wait but each line corresponds to i from 1 to n, so T(n) is the total characters after printing all numbers up to i = n, which includes the newline after each.

Wait, let me formalize this:

Define for each integer k (the i in the loop), the contribution in characters is C(k) = length of the string (either k's digits, or Fizz, etc) + 1 (the newline). Then, the total number of characters up to number m is the sum_{k=1 to m} C(k).

We need to compute the smallest m such that the sum_{k=1 to m} C(k) > N, then the position is N - sum_{k=1 to m-1} C(k). The m-th line's contribution (the line for number m) will include the desired character.

Or maybe the opposite: let m be the value where the total up to the previous m-1 is <= N, and the total up to m is > N. So the target character is in the m's line, the difference gives the position within.

So the first step is to find m such that the total up to m-1 < N <= total up to m-1 plus C(m).

Thus, the key problem is being able to compute the cumulative C(k) sum up to m quickly, so that we can perform a binary search over m to find the correct number, then once we have m, find which part of the line (which character in the string of the m-th line's output) corresponds to the remaining delta (delta = N - total1, where total1 is sum up to m-1). 

The challenge then reduces to two parts:

1. Implement a function that can, for a given m, compute the sum_{k=1}^m C(k).

But how can I compute that efficiently for large m? Let's think about the structure of C(k):

The string for k can be:

- "FizzBuzz" (8 chars) if divisible by 15.

- "Fizz" (4) if divisible by 3 but not 5.

- "Buzz" (4) if divisible by 5 but not 3.

- the digits of k, which has length L digits. Then C(k) = L+1.

Wait the "Buzz" is 4 characters? Wait "Buzz" is length 4, so yes. So the non-Fizz/Buzz lines contribute the digit length plus 1.

The critical part is handling numbers where k is not divisible by 3 or 5, since those depend on the number of digits of k.

So, let me represent the total sum S(m) up to m as:

S(m) = sum_{k=1}^m C(k) 

where C(k) = (len(str(k)) + 1) if k not divisible by 3 or5,

else (if 3 or 5 divides) then:

if divisible by both 15 → 8+1=9?

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Pair {
    int group, prof, c;
    Pair(int g, int p, int cc) : group(g), prof(p), c(cc) {}
    bool operator<(const Pair& other) const { return c > other.c; }
};

int main() {
    int n, m, a;
    cin >> n >> m >> a;

    vector<vector<int>> c(n, vector<int>(m));
    vector<Pair> pairs;
    for (int i = 0; i < n; ++i) {
        for (int j = 0; j < m; ++j) {
            cin >> c[i][j];
            if (c[i][j] > 0) {
                pairs.emplace_back(i, j, c[i][j]);
            }
        }
    }

    sort(pairs.begin(), pairs.end());

    vector<vector<vector<int>>> group_sched(n, vector<vector<int>>(6, vector<int>(7, 0)));
    vector<vector<vector<bool>>> prof_sched(m, vector<vector<bool>>(6, vector<bool>(7, false)));
    vector<vector<int>> classroom(6, vector<int>(7, 0));

    for (const auto& p : pairs) {
        int group = p.group;
        int prof = p.prof;
        int remaining = p.c;

        while (remaining > 0) {
            bool assigned = false;
            for (int d = 0; d < 6; ++d) {
                if (remaining == 0) break;
                int max_possible = min(remaining, 7);
                for (int k = max_possible; k >= 1; --k) {
                    for (int s = 0; s <= 7 - k; ++s) {
                        bool ok = true;
                        for (int slot = s; slot < s + k; ++slot) {
                            if (group_sched[group][d][slot] != 0 || prof_sched[prof][d][slot] || classroom[d][slot] >= a) {
                                ok = false;
                                break;
                            }
                        }
                        if (ok) {
                            for (int slot = s; slot < s + k; ++slot) {
                                group_sched[group][d][slot] = prof + 1;
                                prof_sched[prof][d][slot] = true;
                                classroom[d][slot]++;
                            }
                            remaining -= k;
                            assigned = true;
                            break;
                        }
                    }
                    if (assigned) break;
                }
                if (assigned) break;
            }
        }
    }

    int total_fatigue = 0;

    for (int g = 0; g < n; ++g) {
        for (int d = 0; d < 6; ++d) {
            int first = -1, last = -1;
            for (int s = 0; s < 7; ++s) {
                if (group_sched[g][d][s]) {
                    if (first == -1) first = s;
                    last = s;
                }
            }
            if (first != -1) {
                int spread = last - first + 1;
                total_fatigue += (2 + spread) * (2 + spread);
            }
        }
    }

    for (int p = 0; p < m; ++p) {
        for (int d = 0; d < 6; ++d) {
            int first = -1, last = -1;
            for (int s = 0; s < 7; ++s) {
                if (prof_sched[p][d][s]) {
                    if (first == -1) first = s;
                    last = s;
                }
            }
            if (first != -1) {
                int spread = last - first + 1;
                total_fatigue += (2 + spread) * (2 + spread);
            }
        }
    }

    cout << total_fatigue << endl << endl;

    for (int g = 0; g < n; ++g) {
        for (int s = 0; s < 7; ++s) {
            for (int d = 0; d < 6; ++d) {
                cout << group_sched[g][d][s];
                if (d != 5) cout << ' ';
            }
            cout << endl;
        }
        if (g != n-1) cout << endl;
    }

    return 0;

- B = numbers divisible by 3 but not 15 → each contributes 5.

- C = numbers divisible by 5 but not 15 → each contributes 5.

- D = numbers not divisible by 3 or 5 → contribute (digit_length +1).

Then:

S(m) = 9*A +5*(B +C) + sum_{d in this D}{digit_length(d) +1}.

But digits:

Wait D is the numbers not divisible by 3 or 5 so for each of them, their contribution is (number of digits of k plus 1). So sum of (digits +1) over all those numbers.

The terms for A, B, C are straightforward:

A = floor(m/15)

B = floor(m/3) - A → since floor(m/3) counts multiples of 3 (including 15s). subtract A gives those divisible by 3 but not 15?

Wait wait:

Numbers divisible by 3 and 15 → divisible by 15. So B counts those divisible by 3 but not by 5, so subtract the multiples of 15.

Similarly C is numbers divisible by 5 but not divisible by 15, so:

C = floor(m/5) - floor(m/15)

Because floor(m/5) counts numbers divisible by 5, subtracting A (the 15 divisors) gives those divisible only by 5.

Yes, so B = floor(m/3) - A = (floor(m/3) - floor(m/15))

So total:

the first parts:

9 * A + 5*( (B + C )) where B + C = (floor(m/3) - A) + ( floor(m/5) - A )

because B is floor(m/3) - A (since A = floor(m/15)), and C is floor(m/5)-A.

= (floor(m/3) + floor(m/5)) - 2A 

so then:

9A +5*B +5*C = 9A +5( (floor(m/3)+floor(m/5) - 2A) ) 

= 9A +5floor(m/3) +5 floor(m/5) -10A 

= 5(floor(m/3)+floor(m/5)) - A*(10 -9?)

9A +5(B + C )

=9A +5 [ floor(m/3) - A + floor(m/5) -A ]

=5( floor(m/3)+floor(m/5) ) + (9A - 10A )

=5(floor(m/3) + floor(m/5)) - A 

Because 5*(2A) would be 5*(2A) but I think the math there is correct. 

Yes, expanding terms:

Then adding the 9A gives: 5*(sum) -10A +9A =5*(sum) -A.

S(m) = 5*(floor(m/3) + floor(m/5)) - A 

plus 

sum_{d not divisible by 3 nor 5} ( digit_length(d) +1 ), 

where A is floor(m/15).

<#sep#># Traveling Salesman Problem with Genetic Algorithms in Java

### Introduction

Genetic algorithms are a part of a family of algorithms for global optimization called Evolutionary Computation, which is comprised of artificial intelligence metaheuristics with randomization inspired by biology.

In the previous article, Introduction to Genetic Algorithms in Java, we’ve covered the terminology and theory behind all of the things you’d need to know to successfully implement a genetic algorithm.

### Implementing a Genetic Algorithm

To showcase what we can do with genetic algorithms, let’s solve The Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP) in Java.

TSP formulation: A traveling salesman needs to go through `n` cities to sell his merchandise. There’s a road between each two cities, but some roads are longer and more dangerous than others. Given the cities and the cost of traveling between each two cities, what’s the cheapest way for the salesman to visit all of the cities and come back to the starting city, without passing through any city twice?

Although this may seem like a simple feat, it’s worth noting that this is an NP-hard problem. There’s no algorithm to solve it in polynomial time. Genetic algorithm can only approximate the solution.

Because the solution is rather long, I’ll be breaking it down function by function to explain it here. If you want to preview and/or try the entire implementation, you can find the IntelliJ project on GitHub.

#### Genome Representation

First, we need an individual to represent a candidate solution. Logically, for this we’ll use a class to store the random generation, fitness function, the fitness itself, etc.

To make it easier to calculate fitness for individuals and compare them, we’ll also make it implement `Comparable`:

``````public class SalesmanGenome implements Comparable {
// ...
}
``````

Despite using a class, what our individual essentially is will be only one of its attributes. If we think of TSP, we could enumerate our cities from `0 to n-1`. A solution to the problem would be an array of cities so that the cost of going through them in that order is minimized.

For example, `0-3-1-2-0`. We can store that in an `ArrayList` because the Collections Framework makes it really convenient, but you can use any array-like structure.

The attributes of our class are as follows:

``````// The list with the cities in order in which they should be visited
// This sequence represents the solution to the problem
List<Integer> genome;

// Travel prices are handy to be able to calculate fitness
int[][] travelPrices;

// While the starting city doesn't change the solution of the problem,
// it's handy to just pick one so you could rely on it being the same
// across genomes
int startingCity;

int numberOfCities;

int fitness;
``````

When it comes to constructors we’ll make two – one that makes a random genome, and one that takes an already made genome as an argument:

``````// Generates a random salesman
public SalesmanGenome(int numberOfCities, int[][] travelPrices, int startingCity) {
this.travelPrices = travelPrices;
this.startingCity = startingCity;
this.numberOfCities = numberOfCities;

this.genome = randomSalesman();
this.fitness = this.calculateFitness();
}

// Generates a salesman with a user-defined genome
public SalesmanGenome(List<Integer> permutationOfCities, int numberOfCities, int[][] travelPrices, int startingCity) {
this.genome = permutationOfCities;
this.travelPrices = travelPrices;
this.startingCity = startingCity;
this.numberOfCities = numberOfCities;

this.fitness = this.calculateFitness();
}

// Generates a random genome
// Genomes are permutations of the list of cities, except the starting city
// so we add them all to a list and shuffle
private List<Integer> randomSalesman() {
List<Integer> result = new ArrayList<Integer>();
for (int i = 0; i < numberOfCities; i++) {
if (i != startingCity)
}
Collections.shuffle(result);
return result;
}
``````

#### Fitness Function

You may have noticed that we called the `calculateFitness()` method to assign a fitness value to the object attribute during construction. The function works by following the path laid out in the genome through the price matrix, and adding up the cost.

The fitness turns out to be the actual cost of taking certain path. We’ll want to minimize this cost, so we’ll be facing a minimization problem:

``````public int calculateFitness() {
int fitness = 0;
int currentCity = startingCity;

// Calculating path cost
for (int gene : genome) {
fitness += travelPrices[currentCity][gene];
currentCity = gene;
}

// We have to add going back to the starting city to complete the circle
// the genome is missing the starting city, and indexing starts at 0, which is why we subtract 2
fitness += travelPrices[genome.get(numberOfCities-2)][startingCity];

return fitness;
}
``````

#### The Genetic Algorithm Class

The heart of the algorithm will take place in another class, called `TravelingSalesman`. This class will perform our evolution, and all of the other functions will be contained within it:

``````private int generationSize;
private int genomeSize;
private int numberOfCities;
private int reproductionSize;
private int maxIterations;
private float mutationRate;
private int[][] travelPrices;
private int startingCity;
private int targetFitness;
private int tournamentSize;
private SelectionType selectionType;
``````
• Generation size is the number of genomes/individuals in each generation/population. This parameter is also often called the population size.
• Genome size is the length of the genome `ArrayList`, which will be equal to the `numberOfCities-1`. The two variables are separated for clarity in the rest of the code. This parameter is also often called the chromosome length.
• Reproduction size is the number of genomes who’ll be selected to reproduce to make the next generation. This parameter is also often called the crossover rate.
• Max iteration is the maximum number of generations the program will evolve before terminating, in case there’s no convergence before then.
• Mutation rate refers to the frequency of mutations when creating a new generation.
• Travel prices is a matrix of the prices of travel between each two cities – this matrix will have 0s on the diagonal and symmetrical values in its lower and upper triangle.
• Starting city is the index of the starting city.
• Target fitness is the fitness the best genome has to reach according to the objective function (which will in our implementation be the same as the fitness function) for the program to terminate early. Sometimes setting a target fitness can shorten a program if we only need a specific value or better. Here, if we want to keep our costs bellow a certain number, but don’t care how low exactly, we can use it to set that threshold.
• Tournament size is the size of the tournament for tournament selection.
• Selection type will determine the type of selection we’re using – we’ll implement both roulette and tournament. Here’s the enum for `SelectionType`:
``````public enum SelectionType {
TOURNAMENT,
ROULETTE
}
``````

#### Selection

Although the tournament selection method prevails in most cases, there are situations where you’d want to use other methods. Since a lot of genetic algorithms use the same codebase (the individuals and fitness functions change), it’s good practice to add more options to the algorithm.

We’ll be implementing both roulette and tournament selection:

``````// We select reproductionSize genomes based on the method
// predefined in the attribute selectionType
public List<SalesmanGenome> selection(List<SalesmanGenome> population) {
List<SalesmanGenome> selected = new ArrayList<>();
SalesmanGenome winner;
for (int i=0; i < reproductionSize; i++) {
if (selectionType == SelectionType.ROULETTE) {
}
else if (selectionType == SelectionType.TOURNAMENT) {
}
}

return selected;
}

public SalesmanGenome rouletteSelection(List<SalesmanGenome> population) {
int totalFitness = population.stream().map(SalesmanGenome::getFitness).mapToInt(Integer::intValue).sum();

// We pick a random value - a point on our roulette wheel
Random random = new Random();
int selectedValue = random.nextInt(totalFitness);

// Because we're doing minimization, we need to use reciprocal
// value so the probability of selecting a genome would be
// inversely proportional to its fitness - the smaller the fitness
// the higher the probability
float recValue = (float) 1/selectedValue;

// We add up values until we reach out recValue, and we pick the
// genome that crossed the threshold
float currentSum = 0;
for (SalesmanGenome genome : population) {
currentSum += (float) 1/genome.getFitness();
if (currentSum >= recValue) {
return genome;
}
}

// In case the return didn't happen in the loop above, we just
// select at random
int selectRandom = random.nextInt(generationSize);
<#sep#>-

#include <iostream>
#include <vector>
#include <queue>
#include <algorithm>
using namespace std;

const int INF = 1e9;
const int MAX_BALANCE = 600;

struct State {
    int i, j, b;
    State(int i_, int j_, int b_) : i(i_), j(j_), b(b_) {}
};

int main() {
    string s, t;
    cin >> s >> t;

    int s_len = s.size();
    int t_len = t.size();

    vector<vector<vector<int>>> dist(s_len+1, vector<vector<int>>(t_len+1, vector<int>(MAX_BALANCE+1, INF)));
    vector<vector<vector<char>>> action(s_len+1, vector<vector<char>>(t_len+1, vector<char>(MAX_BALANCE+1, 0)));
    vector<vector<vector<int>>> prev_i(s_len+1, vector<vector<int>>(t_len+1, vector<int>(MAX_BALANCE+1, -1)));
    vector<vector<vector<int>>> prev_j(s_len+1, vector<vector<int>>(t_len+1, vector<int>(MAX_BALANCE+1, -1)));
    vector<vector<vector<int>>> prev_b(s_len+1, vector<vector<int>>(t_len+1, vector<int>(MAX_BALANCE+1, -1)));

    queue<State> q;

    dist[0][0][0] = 0;
    q.emplace(0, 0, 0);

    while (!q.empty()) {
        auto [i, j, b] = q.front();
        q.pop();

        if (i == s_len && j == t_len && b == 0) break;

        // Take from s
        if (i < s_len) {
            char c = s[i];
            int new_b = b + (c == '(' ? 1 : -1);
            if (new_b >= 0 && new_b <= MAX_BALANCE) {
                int new_i = i + 1, new_j = j;
                if (dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] > dist[i][j][b] + 1) {
                    dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] = dist[i][j][b] + 1;
                    prev_i[new_i][new_j][new_b] = i;
                    prev_j[new_i][new_j][new_b] = j;
                    prev_b[new_i][new_j][new_b] = b;
                    action[new_i][new_j][new_b] = c;
                    q.emplace(new_i, new_j, new_b);
                }
            }
        }

        // Take from t
        if (j < t_len) {
            char c = t[j];
            int new_b = b + (c == '(' ? 1 : -1);
            if (new_b >= 0 && new_b <= MAX_BALANCE) {
                int new_i = i, new_j = j + 1;
                if (dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] > dist[i][j][b] + 1) {
                    dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] = dist[i][j][b] + 1;
                    prev_i[new_i][new_j][new_b] = i;
                    prev_j[new_i][new_j][new_b] = j;
                    prev_b[new_i][new_j][new_b] = b;
                    action[new_i][new_j][new_b] = c;
                    q.emplace(new_i, new_j, new_b);
                }
            }
        }

        // Insert '('
        {
            int new_b = b + 1;
            if (new_b <= MAX_BALANCE) {
                int new_i = i, new_j = j;
                if (dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] > dist[i][j][b] + 1) {
                    dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] = dist[i][j][b] + 1;
                    prev_i[new_i][new_j][new_b] = i;
                    prev_j[new_i][new_j][new_b] = j;
                    prev_b[new_i][new_j][new_b] = b;
                    action[new_i][new_j][new_b] = '(';
                    q.emplace(new_i, new_j, new_b);
                }
            }
        }

        // Insert ')'
        {
            int new_b = b - 1;
            if (new_b >= 0) {
                int new_i = i, new_j = j;
                if (dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] > dist[i][j][b] + 1) {
                    dist[new_i][new_j][new_b] = dist[i][j][b] + 1;
                    prev_i[new_i][new_j][new_b] = i;
                    prev_j[new_i][new_j][new_b] = j;
                    prev_b[new_i][new_j][new_b] = b;
                    action[new_i][new_j][new_b] = ')';
                    q.emplace(new_i, new_j, new_b);
                }
            }
        }
    }

    vector<char> res;
    int i = s_len, j = t_len, b = 0;
    while (i != 0 || j != 0 || b != 0) {
        res.push_back(action[i][j][b]);
        int pi = prev_i[i][j][b];
        int pj = prev_j[i][j][b];
        int pb = prev_b[i][j][b];
        i = pi;
        j = pj;
        b = pb;
    }

    reverse(res.begin(), res.end());
    cout << string(res.begin(), res.end()) << endl;

    return 0;

`makedirs` [invocation](https://github.com/CS-SI/eodag/blob/develop/eodag/api/core.py#L98) within the EODataAccessGateway constructor causes `OSError: [Errno 30] Read-only file system` when run on AWS Lambda

```py

def lambda_handler(event, context):
    EODataAccessGateway()
```

**Environment:**

 - Python version: 3.9
 - EODAG version: 2.5.2

**Additional context**

File system on AWS Lambda is read-only. It has "ephemeral storage" that could be accessed in `/tmp` ([1](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/aws-lambda-now-supports-up-to-10-gb-ephemeral-storage/), [2](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/aws/aws-lambda-now-supports-up-to-10-gb-ephemeral-storage/)), however this class does not accept any configuration to where to point to `makedirs`. 

The workaround that heavily relies on the [current implementation](https://github.com/CS-SI/eodag/blob/develop/eodag/api/core.py#L97) is to change `$HOME` env var before invoking the constructor. But it is barely acceptable and extremely inconvenient, since this variable is used by many tools locally and in automated tools along the way.

// We add up values until we reach out recValue, and we pick the
// genome that crossed the threshold
float currentSum = 0;
for (SalesmanGenome genome : population) {
currentSum += (float) 1/genome.getFitness();
if (currentSum >= recValue) {
return genome;
}
}

// In case the return didn't happen in the loop above, we just
// select at random
int selectRandom = random.nextInt(generationSize);
return population.get(selectRandom);
}

// A helper function to pick n random elements from the population
// so we could enter them into a tournament
public static <E> List<E> pickNRandomElements(List<E> list, int n) {
Random r = new Random();
int length = list.size();

if (length < n) return null;

for (int i = length - 1; i >= length - n; --i) {
Collections.swap(list, i , r.nextInt(i + 1));
}
return list.subList(length - n, length);
}

// A simple implementation of the deterministic tournament - the best genome
// always wins
public SalesmanGenome tournamentSelection(List<SalesmanGenome> population) {
List<SalesmanGenome> selected = pickNRandomElements(population, tournamentSize);
return Collections.min(selected);
}
``````

#### Crossover

The crossover for TSP is atypical. Because each genome is a permutation of the list of cities, we can’t just crossover two parents conventionally. Look at the following example (the starting city 0 is implicitly the first and last step):

`2-4-3|1-6-5`

`4-6-5|3-1-2`

What would happen if we crossed these two at the point denoted with a `|`?

`2-4-3-3-1-2`

`4-6-5-1-6-5`

Uh-oh. These don’t go through all the cities and they visit some cities twice, violating multiple conditions of the problem.

So if we can’t use conventional crossover, what do we use?

The technique we’ll be using is called Partially Mapped Crossover or PMX for short. PMX randomly picks one crossover point, but unlike one-point crossover it doesn’t just swap elements from two parents, but instead swaps the elements within them. I find that the process is most comprehensible from an illustration, and we can use the example we’ve previously had trouble with:

As can be seen here, we swap `i`th element of one of the parents with the element equivalent in value to the `i`th element of the other. By doing this, we preserve the properties of permutations. We repeat this process to create the second child as well (with the original values of the parent genomes):

``````public List<SalesmanGenome> crossover(List<SalesmanGenome> parents) {
// Housekeeping
Random random = new Random();
int breakpoint = random.nextInt(genomeSize);
List<SalesmanGenome> children = new ArrayList<>();

// Copy parental genomes - we copy so we wouldn't modify in case they were
// chosen to participate in crossover multiple times
List<Integer> parent1Genome = new ArrayList<>(parents.get(0).getGenome());
List<Integer> parent2Genome = new ArrayList<>(parents.get(1).getGenome());

// Creating child 1
for (int i = 0; i < breakpoint; i++) {
int newVal;
newVal = parent2Genome.get(i);
Collections.swap(parent1Genome, parent1Genome.indexOf(newVal), i);
}
parent1Genome = parents.get(0).getGenome(); // Reseting the edited parent

// Creating child 2
for (int i = breakpoint; i < genomeSize; i++) {
int newVal = parent1Genome.get(i);
Collections.swap(parent2Genome, parent2Genome.indexOf(newVal), i);
}

return children;
}
``````

#### Mutation

Mutation is pretty straightforward – if we pass a probability check we mutate by swapping two cities in the genome. Otherwise, we just return the original genome:

``````public SalesmanGenome mutate(SalesmanGenome salesman) {
Random random = new Random();
float mutate = random.nextFloat();
if (mutate < mutationRate) {
List<Integer> genome = salesman.getGenome();
Collections.swap(genome, random.nextInt(genomeSize), random.nextInt(genomeSize));
return new SalesmanGenome(genome, numberOfCities, travelPrices, startingCity);
}
return salesman;
}
``````

#### Generation Replacement Policies

We’re using a generational algorithm, so we make an entirely new population of children:

``````public List<SalesmanGenome> createGeneration(List<SalesmanGenome> population) {
List<SalesmanGenome> generation = new ArrayList<>();
int currentGenerationSize = 0;
while (currentGenerationSize < generationSize) {
List<SalesmanGenome> parents = pickNRandomElements(population, 2);
List<SalesmanGenome> children = crossover(parents);
children.set(0, mutate(children.get(0)));
children.set(1, mutate(children.get(1)));
currentGenerationSize += 2;
}
return generation;
}
``````

#### Termination

We terminate under the following conditions:

• the number of generations has reached `maxIterations`
• the best genome’s path length is lower than the target path length
``````public SalesmanGenome optimize() {
List<SalesmanGenome> population = initialPopulation();
SalesmanGenome globalBestGenome = population.get(0);
for (int i = 0; i < maxIterations; i++) {
List<SalesmanGenome> selected = selection(population);
population = createGeneration(selected);
globalBestGenome = Collections.min(population);
if (globalBestGenome.getFitness() < targetFitness)
break;
}
return globalBestGenome;
}
``````

The best way to evaluate if this algorithm works properly is to generate some random problems for it and evaluate the run-time:

#include <bits/stdc++.h>
using namespace std;

struct Edge {
    int u, v, w, index;
};

struct DSU {
    vector<int> parent, rank;
    DSU(int n) : parent(n+1), rank(n+1, 1) {
        iota(parent.begin(), parent.end(), 0);
    }
    int find(int u) {
        if (parent[u] != u) parent[u] = find(parent[u]);
        return parent[u];
    }
    bool unite(int u, int v) {
        u = find(u), v = find(v);
        if (u == v) return false;
        if (rank[u] < rank[v]) swap(u, v);
        parent[v] = u;
        rank[u] += rank[v];
        return true;
    }
};

const int LOG = 20;

int main() {
    ios::sync_with_stdio(false);
    cin.tie(nullptr);

    int n, m;
    cin >> n >> m;

    vector<Edge> edges;
    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        int u, v, w;
        cin >> u >> v >> w;
        edges.push_back({u, v, w, i});
    }

    vector<Edge> edges_sorted = edges;
    sort(edges_sorted.begin(), edges_sorted.end(), [](const Edge& a, const Edge& b) {
        return a.w < b.w;
    });

    DSU dsu(n);
    vector<bool> in_mst(m, false);
    long long mst_sum = 0;
    vector<Edge> mst_edges;

    for (const auto& e : edges_sorted) {
        if (dsu.find(e.u) != dsu.find(e.v)) {
            dsu.unite(e.u, e.v);
            in_mst[e.index] = true;
            mst_sum += e.w;
            mst_edges.push_back(e);
        }
    }

    vector<vector<pair<int, int>>> adj(n + 1);
    for (const auto& e : mst_edges) {
        adj[e.u].emplace_back(e.v, e.w);
        adj[e.v].emplace_back(e.u, e.w);
    }

    vector<int> parent(n + 1), depth(n + 1), edge_weight(n + 1);
    queue<int> q;
    q.push(1);
    parent[1] = 0;
    depth[1] = 0;

    while (!q.empty()) {
        int u = q.front(); q.pop();
        for (auto [v, w] : adj[u]) {
            if (v != parent[u]) {
                parent[v] = u;
                depth[v] = depth[u] + 1;
                edge_weight[v] = w;
                q.push(v);
            }
        }
    }

    vector<vector<int>> up(LOG, vector<int>(n + 1));
    vector<vector<int>> max_edge(LOG, vector<int>(n + 1));
    for (int u = 1; u <= n; ++u) {
        up[0][u] = parent[u];
        max_edge[0][u] = edge_weight[u];
    }

    for (int k = 1; k < LOG; ++k) {
        for (int u = 1; u <= n; ++u) {
            up[k][u] = up[k-1][up[k-1][u]];
            max_edge[k][u] = max(max_edge[k-1][u], max_edge[k-1][up[k-1][u]]);
        }
    }

    auto get_max_edge = [&](int u, int v) {
        if (depth[u] < depth[v]) swap(u, v);
        int max_e = 0;
        for (int k = LOG-1; k >= 0; --k) {
            if (depth[u] - (1 << k) >= depth[v]) {
                max_e = max(max_e, max_edge[k][u]);
                u = up[k][u];
            }
        }
        if (u == v) return max_e;
        for (int k = LOG-1; k >= 0; --k) {
            if (up[k][u] != up[k][v]) {
                max_e = max(max_e, max_edge[k][u]);
                max_e = max(max_e, max_edge[k][v]);
                u = up[k][u];
                v = up[k][v];
            }
        }
        return max({max_e, max_edge[0][u], max_edge[0][v]});
    };

    for (int i = 0; i < m; ++i) {
        const Edge& e = edges[i];
        if (in_mst[i]) {
            cout << mst_sum << '\n';
        } else {
            int mx = get_max_edge(e.u, e.v);
            cout << mst_sum - mx + e.w << '\n';
        }
    }

    return 0;

It is measured by satellite radar sensors and allows insights in local precipitation impacts and soil conditions. SSM is a key driver of water and heat fluxes between the ground and the atmosphere, regulating air temperature and humidity. Moreover, in its role as water supply, it is vital to vegetation health. Vice versa, SSM is very sensitive to external forcing in the form of precipitation, temperature, solar irradiation, humidity, and wind. SSM is thus both an integrator of climatic conditions and a driver of local weather and climate, and plays a major role in global water-, energy- and carbon- cycles. Knowledge on the dynamics of soil moisture is important in the understanding of processes in many environmental and socio-economic fields, e.g., its impact on vegetation vitality, crop yield, droughts or exposure to flood threats. 62 3  84 0
